Mutation-checked ten ways in the backend (acknowledged handling, the blocking filter, the step limit, the ring switched off, the no-primary-contact rule, the count, the registry and skill entries) plus one in the Playwright fixture, all caught. Four new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop and phone for each). Full chunked `tests/ask` 1210 tests, 1209 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped; Ask jest 38 suites, 255 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 124 of 124. Not run against a real backend | **Decisions (the recommended definitions, taken per the homeowner's instruction not to wait):** **Renovation (`renovationReadinessProgress`):** the readiness service keeps a state and counts but no percent, so the figure is defined here on its own rule for what blocks a start: a blocking item is settled when satisfied or when its open state was acknowledged; only blocking items count (other open items are listed and shown as a tile, not counted); no blocking items means no ring. Tiles are Blocking, Acknowledged, Other open; the steps are up to three open blocking items with their reason and next action, linking to the case, with no actions. **Home Continuity Plan (`digitalWillHandoffProgress`):** the plan's `completionPercent` is self-reported (the client sets it and publishing forces 100), so it is not used; the ring counts the three requirements the handoff check keeps (an emergency instruction, a primary trusted contact, a way to reach that contact; with no primary contact the contact method is unmet too). Tiles are Met, Missing, Entries; the steps are the unmet requirements in the plan's own words; no entry or contact detail is repeated. `PROGRESS` is allowed for `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS` and `HOME_DIGITAL_WILL` in the registry and the renovation and home digital will skill manifests and evaluations. **Frontend:** none. **Not done:** `tax-readiness-gaps`; nothing on a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 3: folding and pinning results (IW-PRES-021; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.95) | **Implemented in code (frontend only).** Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask`, `src/features/ask` and `e2e/ask`. New test `features/ask/__tests__/conversationView.test.ts` (5 tests: per-session storage, hostile or unreadable values, toggling and reconciliation, the headline and its cut, what can fold). Mutation-checked five ways in the unit code (id limit, the settled-result rule, reconciliation, the headline cut after a test was added for it) plus one against a rebuild (the folded body no longer hidden), all caught. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop: fold to the summary headline, pin to the strip with its freshness, jump to the result, show and unpin, and no further request sent; phone: the strip and a folded result within the screen). Ask jest 39 suites, 260 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 126 of 126. Backend untouched. Not run against a real backend | **Decisions (recommended approach):** **Fold** collapses a result's body to its headline (the summary title, else the first block title, else the question, never generated) and when it was updated, with a Fold / Show button; the body is hidden, not removed, so its view state and any open detail come back as they were. Only a settled result can fold: one waiting on a confirmation, a question, a missing detail or a property choice stays open. **Pin** puts the result on a "Pinned" strip above the conversation showing its headline and when it was last updated, with a jump to the result (focusing its heading) and an unpin button; the result itself keeps its own Refresh, and goes stale like any other (§15). Neither re-runs anything. The state is the execution ids only, per session, in session storage (bounded to 100, ignoring anything unreadable), and a result that leaves the conversation (deleted, or redacted after access was lost) is dropped from both. **Frontend:** `conversationView.ts` (state helpers), `useConversationView.ts`, `PinnedResultsStrip.tsx`, and the header buttons and folded row in `ExecutionCard`. **Not done:** folding all earlier results at once; keyboard shortcuts; nothing on a real backend. |
| Composer: stop, try again and change-and-resend (P0; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.96) | **Implemented in code (frontend only).** Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on `src/components/ask`, `src/features/ask` and `e2e/ask`. Two new mocked Playwright scenarios (desktop: a held request stopped, the question back in the composer with the notice, the late answer discarded; a failed request back in the composer and sent again with Try again; an earlier question brought back, focused, and the earlier answer kept; phone: the Stop button and the banner within the screen). Mutation-checked two ways against rebuilds (no invalidation of the stopped request, so its late answer lands; the Send and Stop buttons sharing one element), both caught. Ask jest 39 suites, 260 tests; `npm run test:ask:e2e` 128 of 128. Backend untouched. Not run against a real backend | **Decisions (recommended approach):** **Stop** replaces Send while a question is being answered. A request cannot be recalled once sent, so Stop only stops waiting: the question returns to the composer (and its draft), a notice says the answer may still appear in the history if the request had already reached Ask, and the late answer is discarded here (its token is made stale, so it never appears and never clears a newer request's loading state). **Try again** is a button on the composer's error banner that sends the restored question; a failed request already put the question back. **Change and resend** is a button under a result's question that puts that question back in the composer, focused, to change and send as a new question; the earlier answer is kept, and the history is never rewritten. **Found while building:** Send and Stop shared one button element, so the click that pressed Stop also submitted the form once the button turned back into Send; they now have their own keys. The button is named "Change and resend this question" so it does not collide with the existing Edit controls. **Not done:** cancelling the server-side request (no endpoint exists for a request that has no execution yet); measuring clarification and unmatched rates (backend analytics work); nothing on a real backend. |
| Display pattern library, step 3: the bespoke domain lists are kept (IW-PRES-012; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.97) | **Decision only; no code changed.** | **Decision (recommended approach taken):** the step 3 delivery note said a domain-specific list is retired once all of its blocks have moved to a shared pattern. The bespoke lists (maintenance, seller prep, inspection findings, radar, rooms, inventory, warranties, household, documents, home events, reserve allocations, buyer tasks, claims) are **not retired**: each one holds what a shared pattern cannot, a live re-read of the canonical record, the corrections and decisions for it, and access-loss handling, and the shared patterns now render through them as layouts (shelves, deck, map) rather than replacing them. They are the domain component; the patterns are how they lay their items out. **Revisit** only if a shared detail mechanism (a generic entity-detail contract) is built, which would be a separate design. |
| Ask orchestrator decomposition review and pilot (architecture; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.97) | **Review written and pilot done.** `docs/architecture/ASK_ORCHESTRATOR_DECOMPOSITION_REVIEW.md` measures the file (20,639 lines, 807 declarations, 111 handlers registered through the capability registry with every handler body still inline), what constrains a split (three value importers, 103 test files importing it, 40 reading its source text), and a phased plan. Pilot: `askFormatting.ts` and `handlers/statusBoard.handler.ts` moved out with the orchestrator re-exporting their names; backend typecheck clean; chunked `tests/ask` 1,210 tests, 1,209 pass, 1 skipped, unchanged. The full `test:chunked` shows failures in `tests/unit` that do not import any changed file; not compared with a clean checkout | No product behaviour changed. Recommended next: phase 1 of the plan, in slices of four to six self-contained read handlers. |
| `askHandlerSupport.ts` split by subject, and an import-graph guardrail (architecture; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.110) | **Done.** The 1,253-line shared bucket became ten modules under `services/ask/support/`: `capture`, `propertyContext`, `executionState`, `answerGuards`, `capabilityDiscovery` (with its `capability.discovery` registration), `commandInputs`, `homeEventCorrection`, `outcomes`, `domainHelpers` (each under 260 lines) and `roomMap`. Code moved unchanged. `roomMapFacts`, which sat in the Home Timeline handler and was used by the Property Summary handler, now lives in `support/roomMap.ts`. `askHandlerSupport.ts` is now only `export * from './support/...'`, so no handler or lifecycle import changed, and the orchestrator facade still exports every name it did before (checked by loading it against the earlier list). New `tests/ask/askImportGraphGuardrails.test.js` builds the value-import graph over `handlers/`, `execution/`, `support/`, `askFormatting.ts`, `askHandlerSupport.ts` and the orchestrator, and fails on a cycle (naming it), on a support module importing a handler, lifecycle file, the facade or the orchestrator, on a handler importing the orchestrator or a lifecycle file other than `executeOperation`, on the facade holding code or missing a module, and on a support module over 400 lines; type-only imports are ignored because they cannot cause a load-time cycle. Mutation-checked: a support cycle, a handler cycle (`homeRecordWrites` and `miscHandlers`), a support module importing the facade, a handler importing a lifecycle file, facade code, a missing facade line and an oversized module are each caught, and a type-only import is not flagged. The source-text helper now reads `support/` too. Today's handler-to-handler edges (for example `miscHandlers` to `homeRecordWrites`) are an acyclic graph and stay allowed. Verified: typecheck, and `test:ask:chunked` 1,228 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped. |
| Calm conversational answers (§11.11, September 25, 2026, FRD v1.111) | **Slices A to E implemented in code and ON BY DEFAULT for everyone (September 25, 2026); `?calm=0` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_ASK_CALM_ANSWERS=false` revert.** Some per-domain list titles and detail flows still use each domain's own layout. | **A** (d81ca7d1) Maintenance headline anatomy. **B** (9aa7ab72) calm shell for every answer. **C** one slim header with a status dot and icon-only delete, no composer helper copy (multiline hint only while focused), history rail without brand block or explanatory footer, follow-up chips docked above the composer (`followUpSuggestions`, `FollowUpRow`), inline chips removed from calm answers. **D** landing: greeting, state strip (`buildConciergeStateStrip`, `CalmLanding`), starter chips, explorer link; the "Popular ways" and "For your attention" cards are not shown. **E** every summary uses the headline anatomy (producer headline when declared, else title as headline and body as ordinary text, thin rule for caution or critical tone), and every domain list loses its outer frame (`FRAMELESS_LISTS`, CSS only; section dividers stay). Verified: frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean; 56 Jest suites / 390 tests in `components/ask` and `features/ask`; **Ask Playwright suite 139 of 139 passed on a fresh `ASK_ACCEPTANCE_FIXTURE=1` build with calm as the default**: every older spec pins the previous presentation through `installAskContext`, and 4 calm specs use the default (landing, answer, domain list, and `?calm=0` being remembered); 9 new helper tests and 6 new chrome component tests. Not verified at phone width for the calm setting, on the live app, or with real data. **Visual pass (after live review the same day):** the global `h1`–`h6` rule (Poppins, 20 to 36px, bottom margin) was making every list title in an answer look like a page heading, so headings inside the Ask surface now inherit the body face and size (`.ask-calm`, scoped, in `globals.css`); the serif is Fraunces 500 at 22 to 30px and appears only on the greeting and the answer headline; each list's own title is visually hidden but stays for assistive technology; the landing shows count chips with a colored dot instead of repeating them as a sentence, labels the one urgent item "Top priority", drops any starter that repeats a chip or that item, scrolls chips sideways on a phone, and uses a warm page background; a calm answer moves focus to its own heading rather than its first button. Checked by screenshots of the built fixture app at 1440 and 390 px. Not done: a bare item card per domain, per-domain headline sentences beyond Maintenance, and dropping each list's own title. |
| Conversational UI principles (§11.12, September 25–26, 2026, FRD v1.113) | **Requirements written; slices F, H and the first part of G (the completion flow) implemented in code.** Slice I (sources chip in the feedback row) implemented; slice J (undo receipts) and the remaining capture forms were evaluated and deliberately not built (see the J and G-remainder notes below). | IW-CONV-001–015 and an audit of two live screenshots (a form-card capture flow and a superseded answer). **F:** in the calm shell a superseded answer is a one-line "Earlier version of this answer · show" stub with no frame that opens the same calm presentation in reduced emphasis; the header status dot shows its plain label beside it on wide screens. **Density pass (same day, after live review of the deployed calm UI):** "What comes next" is collapsed after a main artifact and one row per tool otherwise; the per-answer timestamp line and the summary's duplicate action are gone; on wide screens the second title band is hidden, the status dot sits beside New conversation in the rail (an accessible heading remains), the rail shows nothing when there is no history, the landing's top priority is one line, the hero composer is one row, and turn spacing is tighter. Verified by screenshots of the built fixture app at 1440 px, frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean, 58 suites / 400 tests, Ask Playwright suite 139 of 139 plus 6 calm specs. Not verified on the live app or at phone width for this pass. **G (completion flow):** with the calm shell on, a plain standard-sensitivity workflow capture (`canAskConversationally`: a group of choice, yes/no, number, short-text or time fields, classification WORKFLOW_INPUT) is asked one question at a time (`ConversationalCapture`): the question, then a chip per option for a bounded choice (a picker above 8), a number field with Continue for a value, Skip when optional, earlier answers as "so far" chips that can be changed, and the same single `submitAskCapture` request once every question is answered or skipped. Nothing submits by itself when the card opens already answered (a restored draft or a value the server rejected); it shows the answers and a Continue button. The capture's plain lead-in summary is not drawn above the question, and the review that follows is the existing confirmation restyled as a message (no tinted card, no eyebrow). Backend: `StructuredCaptureField.prompt` (plain question), and the Maintenance completion capture now asks *Which task did you complete?* then *Was there an actual cost?*; the project follow-up outcome question is asked only when the chosen task is a project follow-up (the handler already asks again with the outcome required). No schema, validation, confirmation version, consent or authorization rule changed; property-context forms and every other capture keep the existing form. Verified: backend `tsc` clean, 219 targeted Ask tests (4 new), frontend `tsc` clean, 60 suites / 413 tests (13 new), Ask Playwright suite 145 of 145 on a fresh fixture build including 4 new completion specs, screenshots of the three steps at 1440 and 390 px. Not verified on the live app or against a real backend. **Cleanup pass (after the deployed slice G review):** the refresh-status label no longer sits beside New conversation in the rail (it made the button wrap; the rail shows the dot only); the landing is the greeting, the composer and one row of pills (state chips with a dot, starter questions, then a dashed "More ideas" pill that opens the capability explorer), with no Top priority row and no separate link; sources are one small chip ("N sources") that opens the same context panel instead of a boxed card; the three correction links are one menu beside the thumbs; maintenance filters and the Shelves/List switch are quiet text controls, shelf cards are a fixed size with a one-line meta row, and the answer keeps View all and Create a task while the generic Open and Setup links are dropped; a capture is asked conversationally only when it has three or fewer questions (creating a task, six fields, stays one form), and after a rejected save a Continue button returns so the person is never left without a next step. Verified by screenshots of the built fixture app at 1440 and 390 px, frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean, 60 suites / 418 tests, Ask Playwright suite 145 of 145. Not verified on the live app. **H (visible work):** with the calm shell on, a question is on screen the moment it is sent (`PendingTurn`): the homeowner's message, the Cozy avatar row, a status line, and two quiet placeholder bars in the answer's place (no motion under reduced-motion). The status names an area only from the question's own words (`pendingStatusLabel`: maintenance, coverage and warranty, inventory, costs and finances, weather and alerts, documents; otherwise "Checking your home record…"), never as a claim about server progress. After 6 seconds it says "Still working on this…"; after 15 seconds it says the request is taking longer than usual and can be stopped. The first question no longer leaves the landing in place with no sign of work (the previous behavior shows nothing until the answer arrives): the landing gives way to the conversation and the footer composer, whose Stop control returns the question to the input. The answer replaces the placeholder in the same position (the bubble does not move; checked in a browser). Verified: frontend `tsc` and `eslint` clean, 62 suites / 425 tests (7 new), Ask Playwright suite 148 of 148 including 3 new pending specs, screenshots of the pending and answered states. Not built: naming each source as it completes (needs server progress events). Not verified on the live app. **I (sources chip):** in the calm shell the `ResponseContextSummary` chip is no longer its own line under the blocks; `ExecutionFeedback` takes it as `leading`, so the chip, the two rating buttons and the corrections menu are one row. Verified: frontend `tsc` clean, Ask jest 61 suites / 424 tests (1 new: chip shares a row with both rating buttons, `aria-expanded` false then true, text "Context open"), Ask Playwright suite 148 of 148 on a fresh fixture build, screenshots at 1440 (closed and open) and 390 px. Not verified on the live app. **J (undo receipts, IW-CONV-007) — evaluated, not built (decision, code-traced, nothing executed):** the only reversal that exists is `MAINTENANCE_UPDATE` action `REOPEN`, which sets the task to PENDING behind a confirmation. It is not a faithful undo of a completion: completing a task also writes `lastCompletedDate`, `actualCost`, `completionMetadata` and, for a recurring task, advances `nextDueDate` (`buildStatusUpdateData`); reopening reverses none of these (only the seasonal-checklist counter and Radar reconciliation are reversed), and a project follow-up completion additionally updates the `ProjectRecord`, may create a remediation task and has emitted analytics. A one-tap "Undo" would therefore leave a recurring task pending with next cycle's due date. A correct undo needs a new write operation that snapshots and restores the pre-completion fields (scoped to non-recurring, non-project tasks), with its own compare-and-swap and registry/governance coverage; that is a product-and-backend decision, so no receipt was added and completion keeps its explicit confirmation. **G remainder — evaluated, no change:** every remaining capture is excluded for a reason that would need a rule change rather than presentation: more than three questions (task creation, inventory, claim filing, Radar), or `APPROXIMATE_DATE` / relational (`RELATIONAL_SELECT_CREATE`, `RELATIONAL_UPDATE`) inputs whose precision and create-new semantics a chat step would have to re-implement. Captures within the existing gate (including the deadline and maintenance-date captures, which are short-text dates) already use the conversational flow. **v1.113 landing and task capture (after live review of the deployed app):** IW-CONV-017 and IW-CONV-018 above. Reverses the earlier three-question limit (creating a task is now conversational). Verified: frontend and backend `tsc` clean, Ask jest 424/424 (three tests updated for the new landing and limit), Ask Playwright suite 148/148 on a fresh fixture build (calm landing spec updated), screenshots of the landing at 1440 and 390 and of the first task question at 1440. Not verified: later task questions in a browser, the live app, or a real backend. |
| Conversation lifecycle, context panel and composer keys moved into hooks (P2, architecture; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.109) | **Done; the `AskWorkspace` split is complete.** Three more hooks, code moved unchanged: `workspace/useSessionLifecycle.ts` (starting or restoring a conversation from the URL and storage, the active-session record, the address bar, back/forward, clear and new conversation), `workspace/useResponseContextPanel.ts` (open/close with the history entry and focus return, restore on back/forward and reload) and `workspace/useComposerKeys.ts` (submit, Enter to send, IME composition guard). `AskWorkspace.tsx` 627 to 405 lines (ceiling 450; 2,332 at the start of the split), holding the component's state, wiring and render. Tests: `useSessionLifecycle.test.tsx` (24), `useResponseContextPanel.test.tsx` (8), `useComposerKeys.test.tsx` (3); about 70 mutations tried, the survivors (request tracker not cleared on a new session, the kept return-to result, the once-only initial question, clearing the box on a new conversation, history-entry ownership, property check on back) closed with tests; a few survivors were equivalent or untestable and are not claimed. tsc, eslint, 357 jest, build and 135 Playwright scenarios pass. **Not unit-tested:** the reload branch of back/forward for another home (`window.location.reload`), which jsdom cannot stub. |

All moved unchanged; the request refs stay owned by the workspace. `AskWorkspace.tsx` 701 to 627 lines (ceiling 650). | Voice input in the composer (P2; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.102) | **Done.** A microphone button beside Send (`VoiceInputButton`, `features/ask/voiceInput.ts`) uses the browser's speech recognition. It fills the question box live after any text already there, and **never sends**; the person edits and sends as with a typed question. Hidden where the browser has no speech recognition; disabled while a question runs; aborts on unmount; plain-language messages for blocked microphone, no microphone, offline and no speech; the button's tooltip warns the browser may send audio to its speech service. Input stays within the 4,000-character limit. No new dependency and no server change. Tests: helpers and component with a fake recognizer (7, six mutations caught); e2e with an injected fake at desktop and phone width, and the unsupported case. **Not verified with a real microphone or on Safari/Firefox** (Firefox has no support, so the button is hidden there). **Manual check:** in Chrome or Safari, press the microphone, allow access, speak a question, see it appear, edit it, send. |
| Ask outcome report: clarification and unmatched rates (P1; September 25, 2026, FRD v1.101) | **Tool done; new operations for the top unmatched questions are NOT done and cannot be chosen from this repository.** `askOutcomeReport.ts` (pure `buildAskOutcomeReport`, plus `loadAskOutcomeReport` over top-level `AskExecution` rows) and `scripts/ask-outcome-report.ts` report the answered, clarification and unmatched rates and the top wordings in the last two groups. Unmatched = `OUT_OF_SCOPE`, or a clarification where no operation was chosen. Question text is user-written: wordings are lower-cased with emails and digits masked, and a wording asked fewer than `minCount` (default 2) times is never printed. Tests: rates, grouping, masking, suppression, loader query (five mutations caught). **Next step (needs production data):** run `npx ts-node scripts/ask-outcome-report.ts 28` against the real database and pick operations from the top wordings; nothing has run against a real backend. |

Such a card carries the inventory item's id and the `INVENTORY_ITEM` entity type, because the action dispatches with the card's id; cards with a date, or with no inventory item, are unchanged and read-only. Helper `statusBoardCaptureFields`. Tests: the real handler output through the schema and the answer checker with relevance on (passes as `READY_WITH_LIMITATIONS` because of the missing-date limitation), three mutations caught; e2e at desktop and phone width. **Manual check:** ask for the status board, open a card marked "Install date needed", choose Add install date, enter a date and confirm; ask again and see its age. This closes the "one monolithic renderer" risk (§29) but is a dispatch-mechanism refactor, not new adaptive-presentation intelligence. **Updated September 22, 2026:** grouped-list density, evidence-density, and a reduced-motion accessibility preference are now implemented (see the "Deterministic adaptive presentation" row, which also records Timeline and Form as investigated and closed, not gaps); a deterministic multi-mode selection *policy* spanning every block type together — as opposed to each type's own independent resolver, which is what exists today — still remains required before IW-PRES-001–012 can be claimed complete. A browser acceptance scenario covers access loss without leaving Ask, but was not executed in this environment. |

Read-only: no item `actions` are declared, since no per-item document mutation operation is registered for Ask -- this slice satisfies OPEN_INLINE_ENTITY, not MUTATE_RECORD. The four `capture.*.confirm` handlers were originally registered as `captureNotDirectlyRoutableResult` stubs (`askOrchestrator.service.ts`) with no detail surface able to start them; three of the four now have a declared entry point (`capture.event.confirm` via "Add a timeline event", `capture.warranty.confirm` via "Add a warranty", `capture.evidence.confirm` via "Attach evidence") — only `capture.fact.confirm` still has none, and that is closed below, not open. Phase 3 must not be claimed complete until each entity is either delivered inline (typed action, confirmation, canonical writer, receipt, reconciliation) or recorded as a documented §9 exception — **as of September 22, 2026 this condition is met for every entity** (§9.4 records documents, household role/removal, deletion, the PROPERTY_SUMMARY single-question path, and Room sortOrder/profile/heroImage: PR-EXC-01/02/04/05/06). The one thing genuinely uncovered — correcting a fact that is already known, uncontested, and not stale, which today never surfaces as a requirement and has no UI listing individual known fact values to correct — is a distinct, much larger capability (a new browsable "facts" surface across ~7 areas, not a small extension) and was deliberately NOT scoped as a slice; it is not tracked as a Phase 3 gap unless a future decision adds it; (4) **Room `sortOrder`/`profile`/`heroImage` closed as an approved exception (PR-EXC-06, §9.4)**, not built — none has any traditional frontend surface today, so there was no existing journey to inline. |
| Property records: Evidence upload (Phase 3, add slice, implemented September 22, 2026) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 95 runtime tests in the correction-handler file (10 new for this slice, mutation-checked against two independent mutations: dropping the PRIVATE-creator-only scoping, and disabling the new USER_ADD confirm branch — both caught), whole `tests/ask` directory run (count reported once the run completes), frontend jest (`homeEventResultContinuity.test.tsx`) 10/10; not run against a real backend or database | Reuses the existing `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` operation/adapter/registries unchanged (no new operation id, no registry entries) — only a new USER_ADD branch alongside the pre-existing extraction-sibling branch, matching the design approved the same day. **Byte upload:** a new purpose-built route, `POST /api/documents/property/:propertyId/evidence-upload` (contributor floor via `propertyAuthMiddleware` + a `ROLE_RANK` check, `uploadRateLimiter`, the same `validateDocumentUpload` magic-byte/content-threat check `/analyze` uses), stores via the existing `uploadDocumentBuffer` and creates a `Document` row with no AI analysis and no Magic Scan quota consumed — deliberately not `/analyze`. **Attach:** a new bespoke `AttachEvidenceControl` on `HomeEventDetail` (shown whenever the event has any declared correction action — the same "requester can manage this event" signal `homeEventCorrectionItemActions` already encodes, not a new capability flag) opens a native file picker, uploads immediately via a new `api.uploadAskEvidence` client method, then dispatches `CAPTURE_EVIDENCE_CONFIRM` with the resulting `documentId` and the event's `entityId` through the existing item-action pipe, widened with an optional 6th `documentId` parameter (`onItemAction`/`dispatchItemAction`/`AskCapabilityPrompt['context']`/`CreateAskExecutionPayload.launchContext`, and the matching backend `CreateAskExecutionRequestSchema.launchContext.documentId` field) — every other item action across every entity type passes `undefined` for it unchanged. **Propose** (`evidenceAttachResult`, new): declared-action-only guard (operationId, surface ≠ `ASK_REFRESH`, exact canned message, `entityType === 'HOME_EVENT'`, `entityId`, `documentId` all present) before falling back to the original not-directly-routable boundary; re-verifies the event (same PRIVATE-creator-only scoping as `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT`/`HOME_EVENT_VISIBILITY`) and the document (property-scoped) against live data, never trusting `launchContext`; builds a `NEEDS_CONFIRMATION` card directly, one-shot like `HOME_EVENT_VISIBILITY`, no separate form step since picking/uploading the file already happened client-side. **Confirm** (`confirmCaptureEvidence`, extended): a new `parameters.captureOrigin === USER_ADD_ORIGIN` branch reads `eventId` straight from the execution's own stored parameters and skips the pre-existing extraction path's sibling-execution wait entirely (a homeowner attaching evidence to an *existing, already-confirmed* event has no sibling EVENT candidate to wait on, unlike extraction's paired EVENT+EVIDENCE candidates); writes through the same `homeEventsServiceForCapture.attachDocument` (an upsert, naturally idempotent on retry). The pre-existing extraction-sibling branch is untouched and covered by a regression test. **Boundaries:** one file per attach action; same JPEG/PNG/WEBP/PDF/10MB validation as `/analyze` (both client- and server-side); no detach/replace inline (stays within PR-EXC-01's reasoning); no multi-file batch; an upload that completes but is never confirmed leaves an orphan `Document` row scoped to the property but linked to no event — an already-accepted characteristic shared with `/analyze`'s existing behaviour, not a new one. **Browser coverage added September 22, 2026:** two scenarios now exist and pass in `ask.spec.ts` — one uses `setInputFiles` on the underlying (visually hidden) file input, confirms the upload endpoint is called before Ask ever sees a `documentId`, asserts the exact `launchContext` (entity + `documentId`, never a raw file) reaches `POST /api/ask/executions`, and confirms a zero-editable-field review card (mutation-checked: severing the `documentId` pass-through in `AskWorkspace.tsx`'s `dispatchItemAction` was confirmed to fail this exact assertion against a rebuilt production bundle); the other picks an unsupported file type and asserts the client-side rejection happens before the upload endpoint is ever called. This closes the "evidence needs a file upload" item under the Property records remaining-gap row (below) — `capture.evidence.confirm` is no longer conversational-extraction-only. |
| Property records: Inventory date corrections (Phase 3, first write slice) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, governance/registry tests updated, focused frontend component tests passed; not executed in a browser or against a live DB | New `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `inventory.item-correct` adapter, registered in the operation/command/audience/entity/trust/semantic/skill/coverage registries under the `property-record` skill, which is now autonomy 2 with WRITE effect; Concierge Home is not granted it). The inline inventory detail (`InventoryItemDetail`) shows up to three declared `MUTATE_RECORD` item actions — Correct install date / purchase date / last serviced date — only when the server declares them, which it does only for contributor-and-up on all three inventory producers (`inventory-results`, `inventory-entity-selection`, `property-inventory`). Each dispatches through the normal Ask path with exact `INVENTORY_ITEM` identity via `launchContext`. Propose builds a review card with the current value and a DATE editable field, and performs no write; the existing edit-confirmation path (`editInventoryItemCorrectConfirmation`, version-and-status guarded) saves the corrected date; confirm rechecks a `sha256(id:updatedAt)` freshness version, writes through the canonical `inventoryService.updateItem` (same validation as the Inventory page), and reconciles `INVENTORY_LOOKUP`/`PROPERTY_SUMMARY` via `ASK_MUTATION_IMPACT_MAP`. A retry of an already-applied execution is recognised by value equality and is not reported as a conflict. **Boundaries:** `updateItem` has no compare-and-swap, so a write landing between the confirm-time freshness check and the update is not detected; if no date is supplied and none is recorded the field starts empty and confirm rejects until a date is entered; clearing a date is not offered; no item-level rollback beyond a further correction. Room, document, warranty, household-member and timeline-event writes remain open. No browser acceptance scenario has been written or run. |
| Property records: Timeline event corrections (Phase 3, write slice 2) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, governance/registry tests updated, focused frontend tests passed; not executed in a browser or against a live DB | New `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `home-event.correct` adapter, same registries and `property-record` skill as the inventory slice; not granted to Concierge Home). The inline event detail (`HomeEventDetail`) shows *Correct title* / *Correct date* item actions only when the server declares them (contributor-and-up, on `inventory-history` and `property-recent-events`). Propose lists only current, non-deleted events the requester may see (PRIVATE events only to their creator), writes nothing, and builds a review card with the current value and an editable field; the existing version-and-status-guarded edit path saves the value; confirm rechecks `sha256(id:revision)` freshness and visibility and writes through `HomeEventsService.updateHomeEvent`. **Identity:** `updateHomeEvent` supersedes the row and creates a replacement with a **new id**, so the receipt/artifact carries the replacement id, source lists refresh through `ASK_MUTATION_IMPACT_MAP` (`INVENTORY_LOOKUP`, `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`), and an inline detail opened on the old id resolves to "Event no longer exists" until the refreshed list is used. Retries are guarded by the same `ask-correction:{executionId}` key as `confirmCaptureEvent` (pre-check plus `HOME_EVENT_NOT_FOUND`/`P2002` winner recovery). A date correction records `EXACT_DATE` precision (disclosed on the card); RANGE-precision events are declined for date correction. **Boundaries:** summary, amount, type, importance, visibility, links and clearing values are not correctable inline; the traditional timeline's `correctionReason` prompt is replaced by a fixed "Corrected through Ask after homeowner confirmation" reason; no browser acceptance scenario has been written or run. Room, warranty, document and household-member writes remain open. |
| Property records: Inventory detail corrections and richer confirmation fields (Phase 3, slice 5) | Implemented in code; backend TypeScript clean, 30 runtime tests, routing guard and full `tests/ask` gate, frontend jest 82/82 and Ask Playwright 50/50; not run against a real backend or database | **Confirmation contract:** the editable-field types grow from `DATE`/`TEXT` to `DATE`/`TEXT`/`TEXTAREA`/`SELECT`/`MONEY` (a `SELECT` carries its `options`; a `MONEY` value is dollars with up to two decimals). The values stay strings, each operation's edit handler owns field validation, the API edge's per-edit limit rose from 500 to 2000 characters (long notes), and the item-action limit on a list row rose from 3 to 12. The card renders a dropdown, a `$` amount input and a multi-line box, and shows a `SELECT` value by its label. **Inventory corrections generalised** from three date fields to ten: the dates, `condition` (New/Good/Fair/Poor/Unknown), `brand` and `model` (≤80), `serialNo` (≤120), `purchaseCostCents` and `replacementCostCents` (entered in dollars, written as integer cents, ≤ $10,000,000), and `notes` (≤2000). The fields written are `brand`/`model`/`serialNo`, the ones the traditional form edits (the model also carries a parallel `manufacturer`/`modelNumber`/`serialNumber` set that Ask does not write). Blank values are rejected, so nothing can be cleared inline. The inline detail shows up to three corrections as buttons and folds a longer list behind one *Correct a detail* disclosure. Values compare in canonical form (850 equals 850.00) for the already-applied retry check, and the receipt shows money as `$1,200.00` and a condition by its label. **Shipped gap fixed:** the traditional item `PATCH` controller (not the service) marks five analyses stale — coverage, item coverage, replace-or-repair, risk premium and do-nothing — and the date correction shipped earlier called only the service, so a correction silently left those analyses stale; every inventory correction that writes now repeats all five, and none is called when the value was already applied. **Routing:** the new fields extend the explicit correction-verb pattern, which requires the word *inventory* or *appliance* (not a bare *item* or *system*) so *"edit the notes on this checklist item"* is not captured; `tests/ask/correctionRouting.test.js` now keeps the routing guard permanently (35 read questions that must not reach a correction command, 18 correction phrasings that must). **Boundaries:** room, category, links, `manufacturer`/`modelNumber`/`serialNumber`, UPC/SKU and currency are not correctable inline; no clearing of values; no browser scenario against a live backend. The same field types are not yet used by the event, warranty or room corrections. |

The type list omits `VERIFIED_RESOLUTION`, which the system creates when a guided plan completes; an event that already has that type cannot have its type changed (its other fields stay correctable), and a date-range event still cannot have its date corrected — both are declined at propose time with a clear reason and re-checked at confirm. The confirmation now states that a correction records a new revision and that an evidence-verified event returns to pending confirmation until verified again (that downgrade already happened; it was not disclosed). **Warranty (owner-only, unchanged):** start date, coverage type, policy number, cost and coverage details join provider and expiry date; the two dates are kept in order (an expiry before the start, or a start on or after the expiry, is refused); cost is written as a number and compared in canonical form (450 equals 450.00). Every write is a narrowed single-field patch through the existing writers. **Shared code:** validation, canonical form and display for the five field kinds live in one module (`askCorrectionFields.ts`) used by inventory, event and warranty, replacing three inline copies; the frontend's correction buttons and *Correct a detail* disclosure are one component (`CorrectionActions`) used by the inventory, event and warranty details. **Routing:** the explicit correction-verb patterns extend to the new fields and stay verb-only; the permanent routing guard now covers 45 read questions that must not reach a correction command and 27 correction phrasings that must. **Boundaries:** event visibility and links, warranty linked item and category-specific fields, and clearing any value are not correctable inline; room type and floor level still are not (the operation is a rename); no browser scenario against a live backend. |

The extraction edit path deliberately excludes the date, so an added event has its own form (`CAPTURE_EVENT_ADD`: title, type, date, details, amount, provider). Submission validates (title 3–140; type from the list without the system-created `VERIFIED_RESOLUTION`; date `YYYY-MM-DD`, **not in the future** because a timeline records what happened, with one day of tolerance for timezone skew; amount ≥ 0; unknown fields refused) and builds the review card with the same parameters extraction produces (`datePrecision` `EXACT_DATE`, `attribution` `FIRSTHAND`, the existing capture channel), so the existing `confirmCaptureEvent` / `createHomeEvent` writer handles it unchanged, keyed on the execution id. | Property records: Property Summary completeness rows (Phase 3, add slice 5) | Implemented in code; backend and frontend TypeScript clean, 13 new runtime tests for the handlers (eight mutation-checked) and 8 new evaluator/contract tests (three mutation-checked), whole `tests/ask` directory 837 tests / 836 pass / 0 fail / 1 skipped, frontend jest (`src/components/ask` + `src/features/ask`) 114/114, Ask Playwright 57/57 (two new scenarios; the skip control is mutation-checked); not run against a real backend or database | Each incomplete area on the Property Summary (Core, Location, Structure, Exterior, Responsibility, Systems, Safety) has a contributor-and-up *Fill in missing details* item action that starts the new `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE` operation (contributor floor, confirmation-gated, `property-context.area-capture` adapter under the `property-record` skill; not granted to Concierge Home; non-routable, with one fixed message per area and the row id as the area). The Rooms and Inventory rows reuse the existing *Add a room* / *Add an item* actions; rows for read-only areas (risk, financial, coverage and so on) and viewers keep the link, which now carries the area's page anchor. **Contract:** a new versioned Property Context contract, `PROPERTY_RECORD_SUMMARY:CAPTURE_AREA` 1.0, whose enhancement entries select on the `scope` operation input and reuse each fact's existing capture definition (structured profiles first, because one answer then fills several facts); `VIEW_SUMMARY` and its receipts are unchanged. A registry test fails if a writable fact in the seven areas is missing from it. **Confirmation (IW-CONF-001):** every answer follows form → review card → confirm → receipt; the form is a proposal and writes nothing. The card names the property, each fact and value, and every area affected; the write is `captureFeatureContext` (version-checked, idempotent on execution + requirement + review version), and the receipt names every fact and area actually written and reports *Already saved* on a retry. **Skip:** *Skip for now*, and an answer that is "not sure" for everything, save nothing and are kept in the execution's server-controlled parameters (`skipFactKeys`); a contract-scoped evaluator change honours them only to choose the next question. A skipped fact is never treated as known, never bypasses capture validation (a submitted answer must still be the live evaluated question), and never comes from the client. A refresh re-asks with the execution's own stored skips; a fresh start from a row begins with none; the receipt's *Continue* carries the skips of that same workflow. The same evaluator change leaves out facts that do not apply to the property (for example a private fence for a townhouse). **End state:** never "all done": *No more questions in this session*, the remaining completeness from the same calculation the row uses, how many details were skipped or marked not sure, and the read-only or derived details that need another surface (county, map location, climate region, derived roof age, installed types) by name. **Boundaries:** per-answer confirmation only (batching is a candidate if the flow proves cumbersome); no persisted "not now"; read-only facts cannot be filled here, so an area such as Location may not reach 100% from Ask alone. **Resolved September 22, 2026 (was an open gap):** the pre-existing single-question capture on the Property Summary (`PROPERTY_SUMMARY`, contract `VIEW_SUMMARY`) is still a direct write without a review and confirmation card. This is now a recorded approved exception (PR-EXC-05, §9.4) rather than an open gap: `PROPERTY_CONTEXT_AREA_CAPTURE`, built in this same slice, already puts the seven fillable completeness areas on the full confirmation path, so widening the older single-question path to match would duplicate that coverage. No code changed. |

Whether household members may edit each other's warranties is a product/authorization decision this slice deliberately does not make. **Narrowed write:** the traditional `PATCH` route spreads its entire request body into the update; Ask writes only the one confirmed field. The form is `WORKFLOW_INPUT`, so its button reads *Continue to review*, not *Save*. The warranties list now renders workflow actions through the shared `ActionLink` (as the maintenance list does). **Ownership caveat:** `captureWarranty` assigns the new warranty to the *property's* homeowner profile, so a contributor who adds a warranty cannot later correct it under the owner-only rule (PR-RULE-03); this is inherited behaviour of the existing writer, not new. **Boundaries:** events, facts and evidence cannot yet be added inline; no inventory-item or room add; no browser scenario against a live backend. |
| Routing safety for the correction commands (found and fixed September 21, 2026) | Fixed; full `tests/ask` directory green (783 tests, 782 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped) | **A real regression was introduced by the earlier correction slices and is fixed here.** The semantic router scores by lexical, phrase and concept similarity, and a hard negative can only offset a positive when its evidence exceeds the positive's by 0.05, which is impossible when the positive is already saturated. The four correction operations' examples therefore attracted ordinary read questions: *"When was my water heater last serviced?"* routed to `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT` at 0.997 confidence, and *"What is the warranty expiration date for the roof?"* routed to `WARRANTY_CORRECT`. Fix: `INVENTORY_ITEM_CORRECT`, `HOME_EVENT_CORRECT`, `WARRANTY_CORRECT` and `ROOM_RENAME` are now in `ASK_INTERNAL_OPERATION_IDS`, so fuzzy retrieval never considers them; they are reached only by an explicit correction-verb pattern or by the declared item action a homeowner clicked (which pins the operation). A 34-message battery (24 read questions that must not reach them, 10 correction phrasings that must) passes with no problems. Trade-off: a correction phrased with no correction verb (e.g. *"that date on my dishwasher record is wrong"*) is no longer recognised and falls back to ordinary guidance. **Process finding:** the earlier slices were pushed after running only subsets of `tests/ask`; the full directory then showed six failing tests on `main` (an adapter-count tripwire, three skill-catalog/health expectations, two routing-calibration tests, one caused by my routing fixtures shifting index-derived fixture ids). All are fixed; the legitimate expectation changes (the ASK skill catalog now lists the four commands, the "all adapters disabled" health case must disable them too, adapter count 67→71) preserve each test's original intent. The direct-answer certification objective (pass rate ≥ 0.95) tolerates at most four failing operations out of 81 and already had three unrelated failures (`BUYER_PLAN_STATUS`, `BUYER_TASK_CREATE`, `RENOVATION_PERMIT_READINESS`), which remain and are not part of this work. Also corrected: the answer-trust policy whitelisted only `open-property-record` for `PROPERTY_SUMMARY`, so every Property Summary reported a failed action-applicability check (the block-level links and the new Add action survived only because that repair does not strip `GROUPED_LIST` actions); the real action ids are now whitelisted and a synthetic Property Summary passes trust cleanly. |
| Maintenance inline create workflow | Implemented in code; contract test, focused UI test, TypeScript, ESLint, and production build passed | “Create maintenance task” and “Create a task” are typed response actions that start the existing `MAINTENANCE_TASK_CREATE` capture, review, confirmation, canonical write, and receipt inside Ask Cozy. The source maintenance result is carried through the workflow and refreshed after success; refresh failure is disclosed without inviting a duplicate write. Maintenance Setup and the canonical Maintenance task page remain secondary traditional-navigation choices. A browser acceptance scenario is present but was not executed in this environment. |
| Maintenance inline collection pagination | Implemented in code; focused backend/UI tests and static verification passed | Truncated maintenance sections expose Previous/Next server-page controls inside the same stable Ask result. Paging carries the exact effective query and per-section offset in durable view state, re-queries the full canonical task set, preserves true counts, replaces the prior live result rather than duplicating it, and keeps “View all in Maintenance” as an explicit secondary traditional choice. A browser acceptance scenario is present but was not executed in this environment. |
| Contextual information panel | Foundations implemented for `EVIDENCE`, `ASSUMPTIONS`, `LIMITATION`, exact claim-to-source mapping, two authoritative `OUTPUT_ARTIFACTS` producers, and one authoritative `RELATED_RECORDS` producer; focused contract/component tests and static verification passed | Current response context collapses into one execution-scoped control rather than separate evidence, assumption, artifact, and relationship cards. It opens beside the conversation as a right contextual panel when sufficiently wide and as a dismissible sheet on constrained/mobile layouts, preserving the Ask route, conversation, and composer. Ownership-cost evidence carries server-declared mappings to exact table row identities; dangling references are rejected. Confirmed maintenance-task creation declares its exact canonical task output. Confirmed quote-comparison creation now declares the exact canonical comparison workspace returned by `getOrCreateQuoteComparisonWorkspace`, including whether the record was created or an existing open workspace was reused, its type-specific status and original creation time, and its property-scoped destination. The schema prevents maintenance-only lifecycle values from being applied to quote workspaces and vice versa. Confirmed conversational evidence attachment declares the exact canonical document-to-home-event relationship returned by the authorized, idempotent `HomeEventDocument` write; the client does not infer these artifacts or relationships from labels, URLs, or neighboring content. Completion, reuse, no-provider-selection, and attachment outcomes remain visible in the conversation, while optional canonical navigation is progressively disclosed in context and property-checked by the trust pipeline. Open-panel identity remains bounded per session/property, and archived or superseded responses retain inline disclosures. Broader governed related-record and output-artifact producer coverage remains required before IW-SHELL-006 can be claimed complete. Browser acceptance scenarios are present but were not executed in this environment. |
| Property records: correction browser acceptance (Phase 3) | Two scenarios written and **executed** in desktop Chromium against the mocked Ask acceptance API (`e2e/ask`, `playwright.ask.config.ts`); mobile, the other correction slices and any live-backend run are not covered | (1) A contributor opens a Property Summary timeline event's inline detail, clicks *Correct title*, and the request carries the exact `HOME_EVENT` identity via `launchContext`; the confirmation card's new **TEXT** editable field is edited and saved as a new confirmation version, consent is given, and the confirm request and the completion receipt stay in the conversation without leaving Ask. (2) A result whose items declare no correction actions (the shape a VIEWER receives) renders no correction control. **What this proves and does not:** it verifies the frontend against fixture responses — action rendering, identity dispatch, the TEXT input, the edit/confirm request bodies — not the server's role gating, freshness checks, supersede semantics or writes, which the browser scenarios do not cover. **Backend runtime coverage (added September 21, 2026):** `tests/ask/correctionHandlersRuntime.test.js` executes the *real registered confirm handlers* for inventory, event, warranty and room correction (15 tests) with `prisma` and the canonical writers replaced by recording fakes (the fake `prisma` throws on any model or method a test did not declare; no database is touched). It asserts the actual behaviour: narrowed single-field write patches; the warranty owner check (a CONTRIBUTOR who did not add the warranty is refused with `ASK_PERMISSION_REQUIRED`); stale-version, deleted-record, blank/short-value, expiry-before-start and room-name-clash rejections that perform no write; already-applied retries that do not write again; the timeline event's `ask-correction:{executionId}` idempotency key, replay to the earlier replacement, replacement id on the receipt, `EXACT_DATE` precision on a date correction, RANGE-precision decline, and PRIVATE-event visibility (creator only); and that a room rename repeats the controller's three stale-analysis markers. Two deliberate breakages of the real handlers (removing the warranty owner check and the room name-clash check) were each caught by exactly the matching test. **Still not covered at runtime:** the propose handlers, the edit-confirmation handlers, the real writers (`updateItem`, `updateRoom`, `updateWarranty`, `updateHomeEvent` are stubbed, so their own behaviour and transactions are unverified), and everything against a real database. The first run found two defects in the scenarios themselves (an over-broad selector that matched the response-level *Correct home information* feedback control, and a mocked edit response using a different session id than the page, which the card rejected); neither was an application defect. **Suite repair (September 21, 2026):** verifying showed the full `e2e/ask` suite was already red — 17 of 39 desktop scenarios failed on the original files. It was repaired and both projects now pass (**44 passed / 0 failed**: 39 desktop, 5 mobile, run against a fresh production build). The failures were: (a) **two real application defects the mocks had been hiding** — `MaintenanceResultList` rendered only `href` actions, so the *Create a task* `START_WORKFLOW` action the backend emits for contributors was silently dropped from the maintenance list (fixed by routing non-href actions through the shared `ActionLink`), and the mobile response-context sheet drew the panel's own text *Close* button on top of the sheet's built-in X, two overlapping close controls (fixed with `showCloseButton={false}` inside the sheet; the desktop side panel keeps its button); and (b) **fixture/assertion drift** — responses hardcoding a session id the page no longer uses (now adopted from the create request), a `GROUPED_LIST` fixture missing the `filters` field the real backend always emits (which crashed the renderer), links that now carry a `?backTo=` return context, `getByRole` name matching being a case-insensitive substring, sessionStorage keys that embed the generated session id, a comparison layout control that now defaults to *Auto*, the current access-loss copy, and a mocked recent-session title that no longer matched the restored conversation's question. Scope limits still apply: the scenarios run against a mocked Ask API, so they verify the frontend only. **Correction coverage (added the same day):** browser scenarios now exist and pass for inventory install-date correction (the **DATE** editable field), owner warranty expiry-date correction (DATE) and room rename (**TEXT**), alongside the timeline-event title scenario — each opens the record's inline detail from the Property Summary, clicks the declared action, asserts the exact entity identity in `launchContext`, edits the confirmation field, asserts the exact edit and confirm request bodies (`{ value }`, version 1 then 2, `consentConfirmed`), and sees the receipt without leaving Ask (full suite: 47 passed / 0 failed across desktop and mobile; the identity assertion was confirmed to fail when deliberately broken). Still without browser scenarios: the *non-owner* warranty limitation, the propose-time name-conflict and RANGE-precision declines, freshness/stale-confirm conflicts, and any run against the real backend. Inventory, warranty and room correction flows and the DATE editable path have no browser scenario yet. |

"Mark complete" shows only while the **live** task is pending, in progress or blocked and applies to the purchase; the completion carries the source list for in-place refresh. It is the first Ask operation whose canonical read calls outside services live: Open-Meteo weather and air quality, the county FIPS lookup, the drought monitor and the hardiness zone, each with an 8-second timeout, never throwing (FIPS then drought run in sequence, so the skill's overall latency budget is 20 seconds). The service owns those calls, as it does for the page, so the skill declares no external connector. It is a pure read: the lookups only fill in-memory caches, and the page's room recommendations (generate, save, dismiss, add to home) and plant or zone edits stay on the page. **Source honesty (service change):** every weather signal reads false when its source does not answer, so an outage looked the same as calm weather. `getOutlook` now also returns `sourceStatus` (`OK`, `UNAVAILABLE` or `NO_LOCATION` for weather, air quality, drought and hardiness). The change is additive and the page ignores it. The answer: a summary of care changes due now or soon, plant and garden-zone counts and the hardiness zone, which says "No weather-driven care changes are recommended" only when the weather source answered and otherwise says they could not be checked; a limitation naming each unavailable source (air quality, drought and weather, with "no map location for this home" when there are no coordinates) and saying that does not mean nothing is needed; a limitation when outdoor care is not covered, with the reason from the page's own applicability policy (no private outdoor space, association or landlord responsible, or unknown); care changes grouped do now, do soon and routine, each with the location, triggers, adjusted check cadence, guidance and placement warning; garden zone plans; and a boundary that this is general care guidance, not a plant diagnosis. Plant notes are never included. A home with no plants or zones gets "No plants or garden zones tracked yet", never an all-clear. The page has no care-tab or per-plant deep link (the Care tab only opens for a weather-context or Environment Report launch), so every row links to the page. **Page gap found, not fixed here:** when the weather source is down, the page's own Care tab shows "No weather-driven care changes are recommended right now." It can now read `sourceStatus` to say weather could not be checked. **Routing:** a deterministic pattern (plant advisor, plant care, my or our plants, watering my plants, garden zones, my garden) with an exclusion for choosing new plants ("Which plants should I add to my garden?", "Recommend plants for my garden") and power plants. Card launch: "Show my plant care outlook". **Calibration:** plant phrasing scores low in the retriever, and "plant" contains "plan", so plant text scores close to the Home Continuity Plan. Every candidate fixture with a weak competitor pushed "Show my inspecion findings" to clarification (the v1.53 lesson again). Fixture 080 ("Do my house plants need anything with this weather?", 0.506 vs `HOME_DIGITAL_WILL` 0.678; the deterministic pattern routes it) was chosen by sweeping pairs against every Skill routing case. It keeps every case resolved and moves "Show my inspecion findings" from 0.4322 to 0.4330. No existing calibration row changed. The certified answer first failed relevance (it named a room, which matched the operation's own room-recommendation hard negative), so an answer positive in forecast-care wording was added. The full relevance pass rate went from 0.9592 to 0.9694. **Follow-up:** a `?tab=care` deep link; the page's outage wording; logging watering from Ask. |

The pages ignore both fields. **Page gaps found, not fixed here:** Oracle silently leaves out appliances with no recorded age, and with none left the page says "No Appliance Data"; Budget silently assumes a 10-year-old home when the year built is missing; both services throw plain errors for non-owners, which the pages show as 500s. Ask discloses the left-out appliances and the assumed age, and tells non-owners who can see each tool. **Answers:** Oracle: appliances analysed, critical and high counts and the estimated cost to replace those; the appliances grouped Critical/High/Medium/Low, each with age against expected life, failure risk, years left and an approximate month (or "Past its expected life"), the replacement estimate and the maintenance note; the service's own educational-estimate disclaimer; and the page link labelled "Open Appliance Oracle for AI replacement picks". Budget: the yearly total, monthly average, costliest month and confidence; by category and month by month (routine, preventive, unexpected, seasonal tasks); a boundary that it is a typical-cost estimate, not actual spending; and the link labelled "Open Budget Planner for AI tips". **Page-only by decision:** `appreciation` (Value Tracker needs purchase price and date input), `energy` (Energy Audit reviews uploaded bills) and `visual-inspector` (reviews uploaded photos) produce a fresh AI analysis of something the user supplies and save nothing. Their cards no longer say "not available inside Ask Cozy yet"; they say the tool runs on its own page. **Routing:** Oracle: appliance oracle; appliance or system failure risk, forecast or predictions; appliances or systems likely/about/going/expected to fail, break down, die or wear out; which appliances will fail next/first/soon; past their expected life; closest or nearest to failing. It excludes buy, recommend, brand, model, should, repair, fix, add, record and log, so buying advice and one item's repair-or-replace choice are not claimed. "Which appliances are likely to fail soon?" used to reach `INVENTORY_LOOKUP` through its broad "which … appliances" pattern and now reaches this operation. Budget: budget planner; maintenance, upkeep or repair budget; budget forecast; "how much should/do/will we budget/set aside/save … for maintenance/upkeep/repairs". It excludes create, add, log, record, change, update, set up, renovation, remodel, closing and mortgage. "How much should we budget for home maintenance this year?" used to reach `MAINTENANCE_STATUS` and now reaches this operation. Ownership costs, the maintenance forecast and inventory keep their own routes. Both patterns sit before Do-Nothing, the maintenance patterns and the inventory lookup. Card launches: "Show my appliance oracle", "Show my budget planner". **Calibration:** fixture 077's competitor would have become the Oracle operation; a positive was reworded to avoid it. Four competitor rows were re-derived because the new operations share intrinsic words with them: 005 and 021 to `APPLIANCE_FAILURE_RISK` ("equipment", "appliance"), 008 to `MAINTENANCE_BUDGET_FORECAST` ("budget"), and 087 to `MAINTENANCE_BUDGET_FORECAST`, a tie far below its expected score. Expected scores are unchanged. Fixtures 093 ("Which of our appliances are closest to failing?", 0.270 vs `HOME_STATUS_BOARD` 0.262) and 094 ("What does the budget planner say we will spend on maintenance?", 0.728 vs `OWNERSHIP_COSTS` 0.584) were the only swept candidates that kept every Skill routing case resolved; "Show my inspecion findings" is at 0.4481. The v1.68 Do-Nothing certified answer was reworded after the Budget operation's "upkeep" wording started outscoring it; the full relevance pass rate is 0.9732, and the three failures left were already failing (renovation readiness, buyer plan status, buyer task create). **Follow-up:** the pages' left-out appliances, assumed age and non-owner 500s; per-appliance AI picks and budget tips from Ask (option B, not chosen). |
| Owner-only records: product decision (September 24, 2026, FRD v1.69) | Documentation only; no code changed. | **Decided:** Service Price Radar checks (v1.60), Price Finalization records (v1.67) and Do-Nothing Simulator runs and scenarios (v1.68) stay homeowner-only by default. Their services admit only the primary homeowner profile even though the routes admit any household member; that is now the intended behaviour, and Ask's OWNER floor with a plain "only the home's primary owner" answer for others is final. The earlier open questions on these three are closed. |
| Runtime verification | **Executed and updated September 22, 2026** — the full `e2e/ask` Playwright suite (mocked Ask acceptance API, never a real backend/DB) was run in this session, not just claimed to exist; household, inventory not-found/access-loss/disambiguation, evidence-upload, both capability-card-audit reference journeys (Home Capital Timeline, Home Event Radar), then the TABLE-block row-click-to-detail platform capability added across the day. **Every Property Records browser-coverage gap named in earlier versions of this row is now closed; both capability-card audit reference-journey slices and the TABLE-block platform capability are also now covered.** | `npx playwright test --config=playwright.ask.config.ts` (both `desktop-chromium` and `mobile-chromium` projects, against a fresh production build) — **80 passed, 0 failed** on the last re-run for FRD v1.46 (72 in `ask.spec.ts` + 5 in `ask.mobile.spec.ts` + 3 in `decisionThreadAccessibility.spec.ts` = 80, cross-checked against the run's own reported total, not just its exit code; ten new scenarios added across six rounds, each mutation-checked against a real rebuild — see the "Property Summary inventory and household inline collection/detail", "Inventory inline detail", "Evidence upload", "Home Capital Timeline reference journey", "Home Event Radar reference journey", and "TABLE-block row-click-to-detail platform capability" rows). This covers, by actual test title (not paraphrase): the full-window Ask shell and desktop conversation sidebar/rail (history paging, search, all-home switching, new/restored conversations, draft continuity), the mobile drawer, adaptive table view switching, bounded-comparison strip/show-all switching on desktop and mobile, desktop/mobile response-context (sources) panels, authoritative maintenance and quote-workspace output-artifact context, the document-to-home-event related-record surface, inline maintenance task detail/access-loss redaction/creation/paging, inline Property Summary timeline-event/room/document/**household** detail (including the household-specific "no longer a member" data-absence state), inline inventory item detail's **item-not-found-vs-access-loss distinction** and the **inventory-entity-selection disambiguation list's** inline detail, **inline evidence upload** (out-of-band file upload via `setInputFiles`, exact `launchContext` identity, a zero-editable-field confirmation, and client-side file-type rejection), **inline Home Capital Timeline reserve-allocation detail**, **inline Home Event Radar monitored-event detail** (the capability-card audit's two reference-journey slices), and **inline Home Capital Timeline `TABLE`-row (capital window) detail** (the TABLE-block row-click-to-detail platform capability), the accessibility checks for `DECISION_PROGRESS`/`WHY_NOW`/`SCENARIO_COMPARISON`, and every correction/add/completeness-capture scenario listed in the "Property records: correction browser acceptance" row. **Confirmed still NOT covered by any scenario in the current spec files** (grepped, not assumed): nothing left within Property Records; Home Event Radar notification settings and the two confirmed write cards, Mark done and Feedback. Task create-or-link is now covered; see the "Home Event Radar task create-or-link and notification settings" row; Home Capital Timeline assumption-set editing and contribution adjustment (no traditional-page control exists to mirror; the horizon re-run is covered, see its own row); the other 44 non-reference-journey capabilities in the audit; more broadly, no scenario anywhere in this suite runs against a real backend or database, and adaptive presentation's own remaining scope (a unified cross-block-type selection policy) is its own separate row, untouched by this work. **What this run does and does not prove:** it verifies the frontend against fixture responses on a fresh build — rendering, dispatch, request bodies — exactly as the other browser-acceptance rows already state; it proves nothing about the server's real role gating, freshness checks, or writes. |

## Appendix D — Capability-card audit scope and implementation sequence

The source inventory is `ALL_TOOL_CAPABILITY_DEFINITIONS` in the canonical registry, not the two cards in the reported screenshot. The groups below total 48 registered capabilities as of September 19, 2026. These IDs define the exhaustive **audit population**, not a claim that each capability is shown simultaneously or that each full workflow has been independently verified. `quote-comparison` is workflow-only and must be reviewed at its applicable Ask entry point; the other 47 remain subject to actual availability/readiness policy. A `CATALOG_ONLY` recommendation mode does not waive inline requirements when its card is rendered in Ask.

| Definition group | Count | Capability IDs to classify |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Understand home | 7 | `documents`, `home-records`, `home-digital-will`, `home-risk-replay`, `home-timeline`, `property-brief`, `material-specs` |
| Maintain/prevent | 4 | `home-operations`, `maintenance`, `home-habit-coach`, `plant-advisor` |
| Protect/monitor | 8 | `appreciation`, `claims`, `energy`, `guidance-overview`, `home-event-radar`, `home-briefing`, `neighborhood-change-radar`, `visual-inspector` |
| Decide/compare | 7 | `do-nothing-simulator`, `home-digital-twin`, `negotiation-shield`, `price-finalization`, `quote-comparison`, `replace-repair`, `service-price-radar` |
| Plan/budget | 14 | `budget`, `buyer-closing`, `capital-timeline`, `diy`, `emergency`, `hoa-compliance`, `home-renovation-risk-advisor`, `inspection-hub`, `oracle`, `permits`, `project-tracker`, `reserve-fund`, `seller-prep`, `status-board` |
| Save/optimize | 8 | `break-even`, `coverage-intelligence`, `financing`, `mortgage-refinance-radar`, `ownership-costs`, `property-tax`, `savings-benefits`, `sell-hold-rent` |

Implementation sequence:

1. Generate the §19 coverage rows from this registry and trace all Ask producers/entry surfaces to the shared card contract. For each ID, enumerate the traditional page's normal outcome and all visible controls against canonical services and Ask operations; mark proven inline, missing, or a documented §9 exception. Do not infer completion from an existing answer, link, or recommendation mode.
2. Introduce a typed card primary action with source/property identity and a separate labeled `OPEN_TRADITIONAL` option. Dispatch through registered Ask interactions and retain the conversation/result/draft in the current route. Validate the registry-to-action mapping and reject unclassified new cards or broken targets.
3. Complete the two reference journeys before generalizing: Home Capital Timeline must expose canonical timeline browsing, selection, planning/refinement, and any supported consequential updates, beyond the existing `CAPITAL_RESERVE_PLAN` summary. Home Event Radar must expose its canonical feed, event detail, filters, and supported event/preference actions; do not substitute the broader `INTELLIGENCE_ENVELOPE_QUERY` as if it were the same workflow. Scope each operation against its domain contract before implementing controls or writes.
4. Apply the same contract to every other registry capability and Ask launch surface, completing normal outcomes domain by domain. Until a journey is complete, disclose its exact boundary and optional handoff under §9 rather than silently treating a page link as inline delivery. Preserve existing direct routes.
5. Verify contract/drift checks and focused static/component tests, then desktop, narrow/mobile, keyboard, authorization-loss, stale/partial/empty data, in-flight context change, and explicit traditional-return scenarios when a runnable environment is available. Record which levels actually ran; static inspection alone is not browser proof.
